| 2026-09-18 · albert-two-layer-surgeries-and-lighthouse-ntfy-fix | **Albert grew from 33L to 36L via two Net2Net dual-stream surgeries while the console-driven training run continued unattended, and a separate Lighthouse bug that was drowning Albert's own ntfy alerts got fixed.** The first surgery is fully documented from the live log: at epoch 8711 (2026-09-17 03:31:35 UTC) loss crossed the F9 mastery threshold (3.9983 < 4.0000), triggering `MASTERY EVOLUTION TRIGGERED` → `INITIATING NEURAL SURGERY: Net2Net Safe Copy` → `Dual-stream surgery: 33L → 34L`, with the pre-surgery checkpoint archived to `albert_v3.0.best.33L.safetensors` and the evolution window widening to `ceiling=55L`. A second layer-growth to 36L is confirmed by the live `ARCH` header on the current Modal run (`ARCH 36L 256H 12E 512CTX 32000V`, app `ap-85VcU3vP6tjhxKh2CvT6qF`, started 2026-09-18 08:11 CEST after Simeon restarted training from console), but the exact 34L→35L→36L event lines were not recoverable locally — the rotated `~/.albert/training.log.*` files from that window (2026-09-17 11:40 through 2026-09-18 08:12) hold only Modal deploy boilerplate, not the training body, so those two surgeries are confirmed only by their outcome, with the intermediate event log lost to rotation. Separately, Simeon reported Albert's own ntfy alerts (`albert. WALD`, `albert. SURGERY FIRING`) were getting buried in the shared `albert-rfi-irfos` topic. Root cause: Lighthouse's `audit_anchor.rs` rollback-detection alarm and `tamper.rs` tamper/lockdown alarm both had `NTFY_ALARM_TOPIC`/`NTFY_TOPIC` hardcoded to `https://ntfy.sh/albert-rfi-irfos` instead of a Lighthouse-owned topic — most visibly, a live "AUDIT ROLLBACK DETECTED" alarm (priority 5, row-hash mismatch, likely a backup restore) was firing roughly every 5 minutes and pushing Albert's real signal out of ntfy.sh's ~12h free-tier cache window. Fixed by splitting both onto a dedicated `lighthouse-rfi-irfos` topic (commit `f0c4d1f3a` in the `lighthouse` repo, pushed to `origin/main`; deploy blocked this session — `fly apps list`/`fly orgs list` under the `simeon.feedback@gmail.com` account show no `lighthouse-rfi-irfos` app in either visible org, the documented [[feedback_lighthouse_access_dead_end]] pattern, one check then stop rather than chaining SSH/breakglass/PAT attempts — Simeon needs to run `fly deploy` himself or supply working access. Simeon confirmed the rollback alarm's underlying cause is a known separate issue, only the topic-routing was in scope here); `tower.rs`'s `ALBERT_NTFY_TOPIC` read-path (Lighthouse's dashboard card polling Albert's topic for display) is intentional and was left untouched. A third contamination source, the Gmail-inbox-connector Google Apps Script's `heartbeat` URL, was also pointed at `albert-rfi-irfos` — fixed in `docs/gmail-inbox-connector.gs`, but that file already carried substantial unrelated pre-existing WIP (a ternlang.com→rfi-irfos.com domain-migration pass, v3→v4) in the working tree, so the one-line heartbeat fix was left uncommitted alongside it, kept separate from the ntfy-fix commit; it also needs a manual redeploy to Google Apps Script (`clasp push` or a manual paste into script.google.com) to take effect, since editing the repo copy alone does not update the live script. Earlier in the same session: the L40S training run from 09-16 was stopped cleanly and its checkpoint pulled to local (`models/albert_v3.0.safetensors`/`.best.safetensors`, Global Epoch 8601, best loss 4.019197) so Simeon could refire training from his own console. |
| 2026-09-16 · albert-training-speedup-l40s-and-sync-stall-fix | **Same-day follow-up to the dormancy-recovery session above: a verified-safe logging fix plus a GPU upgrade from L4 (24GB) to L40S (48GB), raising `--batch-size` from 1 to 2 with the increase confirmed by real VRAM telemetry rather than guessed.** A fork agent read `train_bible.rs` end-to-end before anything was touched and found `batch_size=1` was already real batching (`Tensor::stack` into one `model.forward()` call, not gradient-accumulation dressed up as a batch knob — `GRAD_ACCUM_STEPS` exists separately and is fixed at 1) and that it sat at 1 for a documented reason: a 2026-05-29 comment records ~10-12GB of activation memory at CTX=512 on the old 24GB L4, and a stale `_BATCH_SIZE = 8` constant in the same file was calibrated for the earlier CTX=256 architecture and is not evidence that a higher value is safe today — attention memory scales worse than linearly with sequence length, so that number was explicitly not trusted. Mixed precision (F16 casts in the attention matmuls) and the expensive L1 sparsity aux-loss were both already handled correctly (the latter already disabled). The one real, low-risk find: `entropy_scalar`/`lb_scalar` — diagnostic-only values only ever read on log batches (`batch_idx % 10 == 0 || == 5`) — were unconditionally pulled off the GPU with a blocking `.to_scalar::<f32>()` sync on every single micro-batch, for a value 9 out of 10 batches never used. Fixed by deferring both syncs to only fire on the batches that actually read them (`train_bible.rs`, the `is_log_batch` gate added right after the entropy/LB capture drain); the heavier per-variable diagnostics (`global_grad_norm`, `per_layer_grad_norm`, `expert_grad_variance`, `expert_wd_ratio`) were deliberately left untouched because their output feeds real training decisions (cold-layer LR boost, TTL burst-freeze), not just logs — throttling those would change training dynamics, which was out of scope for a "zero-risk" pass. Separately, `gpu="L4"` → `gpu="L40S"` in `train_modal.py` (comment updated with the reasoning and the instruction to verify batch size against real `nvidia-smi`/`gpu_mem_mb()` numbers, not assumptions) after confirming via a live Modal pricing fetch that L40S sits at $1.95/hr — a deliberate step up from the 2026-05-30 "stay on L4 until new funds arrive" budget constraint recorded in that same file, not a silent scope creep. The old L4 app (`ap-mEzSXZZGTgcMFeJgTMsPQ7`) was stopped cleanly via `modal app stop --yes` before the new run launched to avoid two processes writing the same volume checkpoint. Post-launch telemetry from the live `GPUMEM` log lines confirmed the real number instead of extrapolating: `batch_size=2` on the L40S uses 31.8GB of 46GB total VRAM (~13.6GB free) — batch_size=2 is solid, but the per-batch-slot cost is far higher than linear scaling from the old 10-12GB/batch_size=1 baseline would suggest, so batch_size=3-4 was explicitly NOT attempted this session without more headroom or further profiling. Training resumed clean from Global Epoch 8600 on the new config, ~2.1-2.2s/batch, no explosions or OOMs observed. |
| 2026-09-16 · albert-training-resumed-after-41-day-dormancy | **Albert MoE-13 training restarted after being dormant since the last recorded run (Global Epoch 8600, `~1.876M` batch points merged from Downloads); two silent infra bugs found and fixed along the way, neither of which threw a useful error on their own.** `albert-train` failed first with `FileNotFoundError: [Errno 2] No such file or directory: 'modal'` even though `modal` existed at `~/.local/bin/modal` and was on `PATH` — root cause was a broken pipx venv: a system Python upgrade had removed `python3.13` in favor of `python3.14`, so the venv's shebang pointed at a dead interpreter, and the kernel's ENOENT on the missing interpreter surfaces to `subprocess.Popen` as if `modal` itself were missing. Fixed via `pipx reinstall modal` (now 1.5.5 on Python 3.14.4, profile `eriirfos-eng` confirmed live); `pipx list` also flagged `youtube-dl` with the same dead-interpreter issue, left unfixed as out of scope. Second, quieter bug: the local dashboard server (`dashboard/run_server.py`) was hardcoded to port 8888 — the same port a SearxNG Docker container (stood up 2026-09-13 for local OSINT search, see `reference_searxng_local_search_infra`) has bound to since. The dashboard subprocess launched, printed "Dashboard server started (PID: ...)" and exited silently on `OSError: Address already in use` because its stdout/stderr are piped to `DEVNULL`; the browser then loaded SearxNG's own "Page not found" page at `/dashboard/`, which looked like a broken dashboard rather than a port collision. Fixed by moving the dashboard to port 8899 (`PORT` const in `run_server.py` plus the three hardcoded `localhost:8888` URLs in `albert-run`, the actual script behind the `albert-train` symlink). Same pass switched the dashboard's auto-open browser preference from Firefox-first to Chrome-first (`google-chrome`/`google-chrome-stable`/`chromium`/`chromium-browser` tried before `firefox`/`xdg-open`/`open`), per direct request. Training itself resumed clean from Global Epoch 8600 (confirmed via Modal's `latest_epoch` endpoint and the live `training.log` tail: `Epoch 33L (Global 8600) | Loss: 4.0474 | LR: 3.00e-4`), detached on Modal T4 GPU. **Flagged, not investigated:** this log had gone silent for 41 days (last entry 2026-08-06) — unlike the prior 3-week gap, this one was not reconstructed from git history this session; scope was limited to getting Albert training again, not a full retroactive audit of what else moved across TIS repos in the interim. |
| 2026-08-06 · session-log-reconstruction-and-trivago-correction | **A 3-week, 10-repo session-log gap (2026-07-19 → 2026-08-06) reconstructed from raw git history, surfacing a real RTK tooling bug and a stale, materially wrong project memory along the way.** This log had gone silent for 18 days while `rfi-irfos-web`, `lighthouse`, `emergent-interaction-lab`, `coevolution-factory`, `e-techbike-at-live`, `hermes-agent`, `baumhaus`, `ternary-intelligence-stack`, and `veo-framework` collectively took on roughly 650 commits. Reconstructed via five parallel research subagents (one per larger repo) plus direct `git log` reads on the smaller ones, each tasked with clustering raw commits into dated milestones rather than transcribing them one-by-one; `foodsharing` (external upstream, `github.com/foodsharing-dev/foodsharing.git`) was confirmed out-of-scope and excluded, same precedent as `ruflo-eval`/`RuVector`. Two of the five subagents (independently, on `emergent-interaction-lab` and `e-techbike-at-live`) hit the same tooling bug: the RTK hook's rewritten `git log` silently truncates output to ~50 commits regardless of `--since`/`--until` filters, with no warning and a clean exit code — undercounting `emergent-interaction-lab`'s true 133-commit window by 83 and `e-techbike-at-live`'s true 78 by 28. Both recovered the real count via `rtk proxy git log` (RTK's own documented raw-passthrough escape hatch). Logged as a new standing memory (`feedback_rtk_git_log_truncation.md`) alongside the existing `feedback_rtk_exit_codes.md`, since it's the same root cause — RTK's summarization trusting its own filtered view over the real underlying output — just on a different command. Separately, the `rfi-irfos-web` subagent flagged that the site's public disclosure ledger had, on 2026-08-05, corrected a claim that trivago's "Self-Adjudicated Closure" reply had been answered by an R5 sent 2026-07-13 — which prompted a direct check of the actual Gmail thread (full `get_thread`, not the truncating `search_threads`) against this project's own trivago memory. The memory turned out to be wrong in the opposite direction: it said R5 was still an unsent draft awaiting review, but R5 genuinely sent 2026-07-13T10:31 UTC same-day as trivago's closure message, and a further closing notice sent 2026-08-05T17:07 UTC (mislabeled "R5" again in its own header — an unfixable numbering slip in an already-sent email, should have read R6). Corrected `project_trivago_investigation.md` and the top-level `MEMORY.md` index entry to reflect the real current state: case closed on RFI-IRFOS's side as of 2026-08-05, nothing further owed before the 2026-09-25 embargo. Also surfaced, flagged but NOT fixed: `coevolution-factory`'s local `master` has diverged from `origin/master` since 2026-07-23 — 2 local-only commits (`lagebericht.py`, `memory_proxy.py`, real work product) never pushed, while origin gained ~9 commits (the firm/registry TOML overhaul + money-first pricing) never fetched into local, plus a stale `packed-refs` entry a plain fetch didn't clean up — needs a manual reconciliation pass before it's lost, per the standing [[feedback_fetch_before_touching_a_repo]] gate. |
| 2026-08-06 · rfi-irfos-web-hypermodular-scroll-spine | **Built the full "hypermodular" scroll experience Zabih proposed (relayed by Simeon): a center-page spine, a spring-eased traveling orb replacing the conventional scroll-progress bar, a per-card ambient proximity glow tied to the orb's real position, and mixed-direction entrance animations on the Research grid — shipped after Plan Mode design, multiple rounds of screenshot-driven visual debugging, and two genuine root-caused bugs.** New `components/sections/Spine.tsx` renders a fixed-position center rail plus a `useSpring`-eased orb (`usePageScrollProgress()` as source, mapped 3%–97% so the trail never clips at either page edge), fading in only past the Hero and clipping/fading out just above the footer. `Reveal` in `shared.tsx` was rewritten from a one-shot IntersectionObserver reveal into true scroll-scrubbing (`useScroll({target, offset:['start end','end start']})`), and the dead `ScrollProgressBar()` export was deleted outright (zero references, confirmed by grep). The spine initially bled through nearly every card on the site (table rows, KPI stats, buttons) — the first hypothesis (a z-index/stacking-context bug) was wrong and was reverted; the real cause was that card surfaces (`rfi-glass-flat`, the Track Record ledger panel, the featured pricing card, Journey's timeline columns) are semi-transparent by design, so the fix was making those surfaces opaque (`rfi-glass-solid`) at the source rather than adding per-component masking logic to the spine itself. The proximity glow went through three redesigns from user screenshot feedback: section-level → per-card compared against the viewport center (wrong — lit up independent of the orb) → per-card compared against the orb's own live spring position (`smooth.get()`), with falloff tightened from ~440-550px to a fixed 220px, and blur/alpha softened (18px/0.35 → 42px/0.14) after "way more subtle and foggy" feedback, plus a theme-aware `ACCENT_RGB` lookup after the fixed bright-teal drop-shadow read as "a garish green stain" in light mode. Two real bugs found from concrete screenshots rather than vague complaints: `.rfi-proximity-glow`'s own `transition: filter ...` rule, defined after `.rfi-hover-card`'s multi-property `transition` rule at the same specificity, was wholesale replacing (not merging with) that rule's transition list — silently breaking the smooth hover-lift transition on every `.rfi-hover-card` site-wide, including the contact form; fixed by restating the full property list. And `overflow:hidden` on both axes of the carousel track wrapper was clipping the glow's `drop-shadow` filter even though scrolling is horizontal-only; split to `overflowX:hidden, overflowY:visible`. Same push added a "Proof" section surfacing real published PDF disclosure reports as cards directly under the Track Record ledger (cold-visitor UX gap Laura flagged), moved App Privacy to right after Research, fixed a delivery-pill/button wrap bug via shortened copy (kept side-by-side per explicit instruction), and changed "Book us" → "Book us!" in both languages. 14 commits, `npx tsc -b` + `npm run build` + `npm run lint` clean, deployed via `fly deploy`, live bundle hash verified against `rfi-irfos.com`, pushed to `origin/master`. |
| 2026-08-06 · lighthouse-p0-validation-campaign-closed | **Full pre-migration Lighthouse audit found 26 P0-severity findings across two workflow runs; all 26 fixed, tested, and deployed to production the same day, ahead of the 2026-08-08/09 full-life migration.** Simeon's lead volume is climbing and the plan is to run 100% of RFI-IRFOS's work and personal life on Lighthouse starting that weekend, which triggered a hard-deadline, READ_ONLY, static-analysis pre-engineering audit (no live click-throughs, since Lighthouse's hash-chained audit tables make any live interaction a permanent, non-deletable trace). Phases 1-3 (Architecture/Relationship-Mapping/Behaviour, 10 agents) found 69 findings including 14 P0s: broken RBAC across Finance/Marketing/Dashboards/Research/hiring, a nonexistent "write" role, inverted inbox `can_write` polarity, a WOPI IDOR on private docs, private-upload broadcast leaks, and — most consequential — several Bin/soft-delete cascade gaps where deleting a parent record (meetings, member removal, calendar events, dashboard widgets, doc folder subtrees) silently orphaned or destroyed child rows instead of snapshotting them for restore. Phases 4-7 (Stress/Red-Team/Consistency, 35 agents) then found 40 more findings including 12 additional P0s and graded the system release-readiness **F**, with four disqualifying blockers: a CORS misconfiguration (`allow_credentials(true)` + `AllowMethods::any()`) that hard-crashed tower-http 0.6 at router construction, forcing prod to boot only via a wide-open-CORS dev flag; a 2FA bug where the revocation guard treated `totp_pending` as a revoked-role mismatch and wiped the session cookie on the SPA's own bootstrap call, locking out every 2FA-enabled account before they ever reached the code screen (a second latent bypass found and fixed alongside it: the sliding-renewal branch would have silently minted a full session through that same exemption); the "archived" (offboarded) role remaining fully write-capable everywhere despite the UI's "access revoked immediately" claim; and GDPR erasure silently no-op'ing against the immutable `activity` table, with the audit log falsely recording "pseudonymised" while the underlying trigger had rejected the UPDATE outright — a live, ongoing Art. 17 compliance failure. All four blockers were fixed same-day (CORS reproduced and verified in an isolated pinned-dependency project before shipping; GDPR fix adds a narrow one-way `activity.redacted_at` trigger exception via migration 0171, verified against a live throwaway Postgres plus the project's own `backend/tests/safeguards.rs` suite — Simeon's explicit policy call: redact PII content in place rather than delete rows, so nothing silently disappears from the audit trail), deployed via `fly deploy -a lighthouse-rfi-irfos` with all 171 migrations applying cleanly and health checks passing, and pushed to `origin/main`. Explicitly NOT fixed, flagged for the next session: stash+delete across 15+ callers isn't one DB transaction yet, plus the remaining 12 confirmed P1s and ~16 unverified P2/P3s (broken CSV exports, a break-glass 2FA bypass, plaintext TOTP storage, incomplete GDPR export coverage). |

| 2026-08-05 · baumhaus-games-platform-launch | **New private repo: Baumhaus, a real-time two-user multiplayer game platform for Simeon and Laura, built start-to-finish in one ~10-hour push (2026-08-05 21:54 → 2026-08-06 07:49, 41 commits) with Chat, a cross-game Leaderboard, a working Hangman implementation, and a Stadt-Land-Fluss implementation planned.** Design spec and implementation plan committed first, then a Vite/React/TS/Tailwind scaffold wired to Supabase: a two-user row-level-security schema gated on `app_metadata` (not `user_metadata`, corrected same-session after the distinction was flagged — `user_metadata` is client-writable and would have let either player forge the other's identity), a `useAuth` hook with a login-rejection race condition fixed via a re-entrancy guard, and realtime broadcast/presence hooks with a channel-teardown leak fixed via memoization. A same-day security pass made realtime channels private and added signature verification before Hangman itself was built: pure game logic, a Supabase data layer, a game-page component, and a real-money-adjacent match-result-recording bug fixed (wrong expected winner in two separate commits) before wiring `/game/hangman` to the real component. All commits authored by zabih-sudo; `.worktrees/` and the SDD scratch workspace excluded from git and from the Vitest test run. |

Separately, the rendered PDF was flagged by the user as "not richtext" — root cause: `build_report.py`'s primary PDF engine, `pandoc --pdf-engine=pdflatex`, was silently discarding all real CSS (colors, card borders, colored badges) and falling back to a plain default-serif document while still reporting success (exit 0) — a purely visual regression no exit-code/pdftotext check could ever catch, only visual `pdftoppm`+image inspection surfaced it (same lesson as the strategy-pipeline table-divider bug earlier this week). Fixed by making headless Chrome (`--headless=new --print-to-pdf`) the primary engine, since it renders real HTML+CSS like a browser; weasyprint/soffice/pandoc kept only as fallbacks. | 2026-07-16 · lauras-agents | **MISSION CONTROL turntable — first validated live run over a real dossier; ternary context gate designed next.** Private repo `rfi-irfos/lauras-agents`. Context: scaled the 18 hand-authored core agents into a **292-agent data-driven enterprise pool** (TOML `system_prompt`-only manifests loaded at runtime by `DataBackedAgent`; 39 lanes across the org map). Shipped a **source-free public promo shell** at `github.com/rfi-irfos/lauras-agents-public` (README reframed from "document reviewer" to *metacognitive recursive asynchronous multi-agent pipeline*; `docs/agents.md` interlinks all 292; `scripts/guard.sh` pre-commit + CI hook hard-blocks any manifest/crate/prompt from ever entering the public tree — verified it aborts on simulated leaks; proprietary logic stays private, no git-history leak because the public repo is a fresh init). **─── THE 429 LESSON ───** Brute-parallel spawn of all 292 agents over `00_ERSTASSESSMENT.md` (19.5 KB) does NOT work on NVIDIA NIM free-tier: at concurrency=20, 282/292 returned `HTTP 429 Too Many Requests`; at concurrency=3 with exponential backoff, only 9× 429 but **283/292 came back `complete=false` empty** — NIM *soft-throttles* under sustained load, emitting truncated/empty completions rather than hard errors, and single-shot findings proved nondeterministic (legal-ip returned 2 findings then 0 on identical input). Conclusion: burst parallelism is dead for this key tier. **─── THE TURNTABLE (Simeon's design) ───** Reframed the whole execution model around a railway roundhouse metaphor (Ringlokschuppen + Drehscheibe): **ONE turntable, ONE key, ONE locomotive active at a time** — each agent gets the *full* key juice and full reasoning, fed serially, no burst. New crate `lauras-agents-mission`: (a) **context-aware ordering** — a classifier reads the input and picks which lanes run, keyed off signal keywords (appsec→Security/Cyber, gdpr/human-rights→Legal/Risk/HR, ip/klage/staatsanwalt→Legal/Exec, website→Design/Brand, ai-act→AI-Safety/ML); (b) serial async `run()` injecting a shared `master_context` into every agent while each keeps its own sub-prompt; (c) `learn_from_mistakes()` that ticks a gradual checklist and writes the mission YAML back. Wired `POST /mission/run` into the API, persisting `mission_state.json` for the co-host review. `mission_control.yaml` is the conductor's sheet (objective + master_context + classifier + 30 agents + checklist). **─── FIRST LIVE RUN (validated) ───** Ran the turntable over the Laura dossier (`00_ERSTASSESSMENT.md`, a Status-0 legal/evidentiary first-assessment of a real ongoing case): **11 min wall (12:23→12:34Z), 30 locomotives, 0 errored** (vs. 283 broken under brute spawn — the turntable *solved* the throttle problem). 14 agents active, 16 correctly silent (no noise from irrelevant lanes), **47 findings**, and **evidence discipline held: 58/59 findings quoted verbatim from the source (98%)** — one stray. Legal lane led with 20 findings (personal-data-without-lawful-basis with names quoted; the *"unverified claims about individuals"* re: a named officer that a prior metacog reflection had flagged now surfaced automatically); risk-gdpr cited concrete Art. 5 / Art. 32 DSGVO; sece-ng-appsec flagged unredacted docs + private email for official comms. **─── NEXT (designed, not yet built) ───** Simeon's call: we do NOT want "better ordering" — we want an **additional ternary context gate** — a pre-step that scores prompt/input **ambiguity on a scale** and returns affirm/tend/reject on *how clear vs. unclear the request is*, so the pipeline can hold (Tend) and ask rather than charge ahead on a vague brief. Plus the full hardened review loop: ternary gate (`trit_decide`/MoE-13) + Last-Look-Back protocol + call-laura gate before any action executes. Co-hosts: Simeon (organic) + Hermes (digital), reviewing only after ALL payloads return. Note: dossier text was sent to NIM (US-external) on the isolated agentic ZBook per explicit consent; findings are model output, not legal advice — human oversight is the point. 36 Rust tests pass across core+registry+metacog+mission+api. |
| 2026-06-21 · albert | **CTX 256→512 — live on Modal, training confirmed healthy.** Bumped `max_seq_len` from 256 to 512 in `models/albert_v3.0.config.json`. On restart, `recalibrate_ctx(512)` fired automatically: `mastery_threshold` adjusted 4.5→4.0, `plateau_threshold` scaled proportionally by ratio `new_init/old_init` (0.018/0.020 = ×0.90), `min_plateau_threshold` tightened to 0.007. No weight changes needed — albert. has no positional embeddings so CTX is pure truncation. Training resumed at ep7592, 33L, loss ~6.0/batch (expected restart elevation; epoch average will settle). GPU VRAM: 18996MB/23034MB, ample headroom. DIVWD ratios show 0.00e0 in first epoch as AdamW momentum buffers recalibrate to 512-token sequence scale — loss varying between steps confirms gradients are flowing normally. Next surgery gate: mastery fires at 4.0 nats avg (was 4.5), plateau at ~0.016 (decayed from 0.018 initial). Team announcement drafted. |
| 2026-06-21 · albert | **CTX-AWARE EVOLUTION GATE — `CTX_LEVEL_TABLE` + `recalibrate_ctx()`.** Diagnosed that `mastery_threshold=4.5` and `plateau_threshold` were calibrated for CTX=128 and silently drift as the context window grows. Fix: added `CTX_LEVEL_TABLE` in `evolution.rs` with 15 scripted levels from CTX=64 to CTX=1M (end-goal), each row defining `(mastery_threshold, plateau_init, min_plateau_threshold)`. Mastery decreases ~0.3–0.5 nats per CTX doubling (longer context lowers per-token cross-entropy mechanically; same absolute loss at 512-CTX does not represent the same mastery as at 256-CTX). `min_plateau_threshold` decreases with CTX because longer sequences average over more token positions per batch, compressing the epoch-noise floor. Added `with_ctx(max_seq_len)` constructor; `new()` is a backward-compat alias for `with_ctx(256)`. Added `recalibrate_ctx(new_ctx)` — safe to call every restart: same CTX = no-op (refreshes mastery from table); CTX bump = scales current `plateau_threshold` proportionally (`ratio = new_init/old_init`, clamped to `[new_min, new_init]`) so generational decay is preserved across the boundary. `advance_generation` now uses `self.min_plateau_threshold` instead of a hardcoded const, so the floor stays appropriate for the active CTX. `save_state`/`load_state` persist `ctx:` and `mt:` as tagged lines — fully backward-compatible with old state files (missing tags → defaults). `train_bible.rs`: `EvolutionManager::new()` replaced with `with_ctx(initial_ctx)` reading `max_seq_len` from `config.json` at startup; `recalibrate_ctx(initial_ctx)` called after `load_state` to apply any CTX change. Also fixed pre-existing `ste.rs` compile error (missing `activation_checkpoint_interval` field in `TransformerConfig` literal). **44/44 tests pass.** Current live state: ep7588, ATL 4.6842, 33L·256H·12E·2-stream·256CTX, WALD severity=0.000, mastery gate fires at 4.5 (~37 epochs out). |
| 2026-06-19 · lighthouse | **Split-flap clock, interactive onboarding tour rewrite, global keyboard shortcuts (deploying v442).** Three major features in one batch. **(1) Solari split-flap clock:** every digit in the WorldClock "Flip" view now flips independently like a stadium departure board — `SplitFlapDigit` tracks prev/cur, fires a two-phase 3D CSS animation: top half folds away (`rotateX 0→-90deg`, 160ms ease-in), bottom half falls in (`rotateX 90→0deg`, 160ms ease-out, 160ms delay). Colon rendered as two round pill dots (no flip). Only changed digits animate — when just the last-seconds digit ticks, the hour and minute cards stay completely still. **(2) Onboarding tour complete rewrite:** new architecture with three phases — **Entry choice screen** (Full tour vs Pick an app), **App picker grid** (20 apps, per-app SVG icons, 3-column chip grid), **Active tour** (element-level spotlight + tooltip). Spotlight mechanism: 4 absolutely-positioned dark overlay rects create a real cutout around the target element, with a pulsing teal glow ring on top. Tooltip auto-positions left/right/top/bottom based on available space. Two step types: `intro` (full-screen centered card, no spotlight, for app welcome screens) and `element` (spotlight on a specific CSS selector). Baywatch has 4 steps with on-page element spotlights (`data-tour="baywatch-albert"`, `data-tour="baywatch-pulse"`, `data-tour="topbar-search"`). Keyboard navigation: Arrow keys / Enter / Escape during the tour. Progress dots bottom-center, clickable. **(3) Global keyboard shortcuts:** `Ctrl+0` relaunch tour, `Ctrl+L` → Leads (/mail), `Ctrl+J` → open Comms panel, `Ctrl+/` → toggle sidebar, `Ctrl+1–9` → jump to nth nav item. Blocked while typing in input/textarea/contenteditable. **(4) Migration 124 fix:** `tickets.description` → `tickets.body` — was the root cause of 3 consecutive failed deploys (migration gate held prod on v441 each time). **(5) TS6133 fix:** unused `st` variable in `tipPos()` caught by Docker's strict tsc (not local). |

| 2026-06-19 · lighthouse | **Audit wave 2 — four real bugs found by agent sweep of Meetings, Finance, Comms, AlbertChat.** Background audit agent read all five remaining route files and surfaced four issues. **(1) Meeting join-button thundering herd (HIGH):** `joinMut.isPending` was a single shared boolean — clicking join on any one meeting disabled ALL join buttons simultaneously. Fixed by adding `const [joiningId, setJoiningId] = useState<number | null>(null)`, setting it before each mutation, and passing `joining={joinMut.isPending && joiningId === m.id}` so only the clicked row shows its own pending state. **(2) Finance ledger delete button (HIGH):** the trash icon called `DELETE /api/finance/ledger/:id` but the cash_ledger table has an append-only DB trigger (migration 0108) that blocks all DELETE operations — every click produced a silent error toast with no action. Replaced with a "Reverse" button (swap-arrows icon, hover accent) that calls the existing `ledger_delete` endpoint which posts an idempotent contra-entry via `ON CONFLICT (ref_type, ref_id) WHERE ref_type IS NOT NULL DO NOTHING`. The ledger stays append-only, corrections are auditable. **(3) Comms typing indicator (LOW):** `onInputKey()` fired `signalTyping(channelId)` unconditionally — while on the DMs tab, channelId still held the last-visited channel, so every keystroke in a DM sent phantom typing events to that channel. Guarded: `if (tab !== 'channels' | channelId == null) return`. **(4) AlbertChat 429 (LOW):** all errors stringified generically, so hitting the AI rate cap showed "Error: 429: Too Many Requests". Now: `s.includes('429') ? 'Rate limit reached — wait a moment and try again.' : s`. Tasks.tsx verified clean; no issues found. All four fixes committed in one batch. |
| 2026-06-19 · lighthouse | **Production-scale audit — 12 correctness and scale fixes across the full backend (single batch deploy).** After the directive "no new deploy until everything is through", a systematic audit of all Lighthouse backend modules found and fixed: **(1) N+1 worktime team endpoint** — `account_for()` called per team member produced ~350 DB queries for 50 members; replaced with 7 batch queries using `= ANY($1)` binding and Rust-side aggregation. **(2) Thundering herd** — all 50 clients refetched dashboards at identical 30s intervals; fixed with `useJitteredInterval(30000, 15000)` (a stable `useRef` random spread per client mount). **(3) SSE broadcast buffer** — `broadcast::channel(512)` in `comms.rs` could drop messages under burst load; raised to 4096 for both CHAN_TX and DM_TX. **(4) Audit chain OOM** — `verify_chain` called `fetch_all()` on the entire audit_log (millions of rows → OOM); replaced with paged 5000-row `LIMIT/OFFSET` streaming with a labeled `break 'outer` loop. **(5) AI spend cap** — added `AI_BUCKETS` (per-user 30 req/hr sliding window) and `AI_DAILY` (2000/day global cap) in `ai.rs`; quota checked in `chat()` before any processing. **(6) Albert enum validation** — `exec_writes()` now validates ticket/task statuses, priorities, and deal stages against allowlists before touching the DB; invalid values return a descriptive error instead of silently writing bad data. **(7) Payroll TOCTOU race** — check→delete→rebuild was not atomic; wrapped in `db.begin()` + `SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext($1)::bigint)` per period. **(8) Storno partial failure** — three-step storno (create storno + copy negated items + void original) could leave orphaned invoices; wrapped in a transaction. **(9) Tasks unbounded list** — `list_tasks()` had no LIMIT; added `LIMIT 500`. **(10) Ideas N+1 + pagination** — per-row `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM activity` replaced with a `LEFT JOIN … GROUP BY` count; pagination added (`?page&per`, default 50, max 200). **(11) JourneyPanel + QuickNotes poll rates** — 3s/0s → 15s/5s and 5s/0s → 30s/15s staleTime. **(12) SSE DM routing** — `comms.rs` was querying wrong column (`cm.username` instead of `cm.member`) and wrong table (`dms` instead of `direct_messages`) for DM real-time messages; DMs never arrived live before this fix. All 12 shipped in one batch commit, 0 cargo errors. |
| 2026-06-19 · lighthouse | **Two new migrations + search index compatibility fix.** **(1) Migration 0124 — pg_trgm GIN indexes:** `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm` + GIN trigram indexes on inbox (subject/from_name/from_addr), contacts (name/email/org), deals (title), tickets (title/description), docs (name), datasets (name), messages (body) — all on `lower(col) gin_trgm_ops`. **(2) Migration 0125 — received invoices dedup:** `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS received_invoices_supplier_num ON received_invoices (supplier_name, invoice_number) WHERE invoice_number IS NOT NULL AND invoice_number != ''` — prevents the same supplier invoice from being imported twice. **(3) search.rs LIKE pattern fix:** the old `fuzzy_like()` function produced subsequence patterns (`%r%e%a%d%m%e%`) that Postgres trigram indexes can't use (trigrams require ≥3 consecutive chars). | 2026-06-17 · training | **albert training loop optimized — GRAD_ACCUM_STEPS 4→1, CTX 128→256, CHANGELOG purged.** Three training config changes on the fresh restart at ep~6730 (ATL 5.4318 @ ep6682, post-S19 32L). **(1) GRAD_ACCUM_STEPS 4→1** (`train_bible.rs`): accumulation was calibrated for a smaller model to stabilize gradients via a larger effective batch. At 32L + dual-stream + 12 experts, each single forward pass already produces gradients across 384 expert pathways — accumulating over 4 micro-batches adds no stability benefit, only a compute spike every 4th batch ("ruckling" visible in log and telemetry). With ACCUM=1, every batch is an immediate optimizer step, no ruckling. **(2) max_seq_len 128→256** (`albert_v3.0.config.json`): context was conservatively low; T4 16GB has ample headroom at the current model size (~232M params, ~2.4GB params+moments), doubling context to 256 stays well within VRAM budget. **(3) batch_size 1** (confirmed — modal launch arg): kept at 1 per micro-batch; with CTX=256 and ACCUM=1, effective throughput is 256 tokens/step, up from 128 tokens/step (4×128/4=128) before. **Also:** CHANGELOG.md purged entirely — `docs/session_log.md` is the sole ledger; CHANGELOG now just points here. |

| 2026-06-05 · 14:00 | **Lighthouse OS — EU audit sweep, governance quorum system, and structural immutability (the OS now protects itself from its own CEO).** Three interlocking layers shipped in one session. **1. EU activity logging sweep:** every write path across all 8 backend modules (`calendar`, `finance`, `meetings`, `ideas`, `invoices`, `marketing`, `docs`, `tasks`) now calls `crate::activity::log` with the real actor username and field-level diff. All handlers that discarded the session username via `Some((_, role))` were corrected to `Some((user, role))`. Delete operations fetch the record's display name before the DELETE so the log entry is human-readable. This closes the EU AI Act / 5-year retention mandate at the application layer. **2. Emergency governance quorum:** a full multi-party authorization system (`governance.rs`, migration `0080`) for any privileged mutation. Design principle stated by Simeon: "i cannot be a single point of failure, and neither can i be corrupted." Implementation: `governance_proposals` + `governance_votes` tables; proposals require unanimous approval from ALL current admins (minimum 3 required — the system refuses to arm with fewer); each vote is irrevocable and chains cryptographically into the previous via SHA-256 (`sha256(prev_hash ∥ voter ∥ timestamp)`); the root hash anchors the proposal content; a `final_hash` seals the chain at execution and is written to `audit_log` with the full vote chain in `meta`. Proposals expire after 72 hours. A dedicated `Governance` tab in Settings (admin-only) shows pending proposals with a progress bar, each voter's name as a green chip with their vote hash in the tooltip, and a two-step inline confirm before approving. Empty state: "No proposals — good. This tab should stay empty." **3. DB-level immutability (structural, not convention):** PostgreSQL `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` and `BEFORE TRUNCATE` triggers on `activity`, `audit_log`, and `governance_votes` throw `restrict_violation` before touching a single row. Any `UPDATE` or `DELETE` — even from a raw `psql` superuser session — is structurally impossible. Bypassing requires `DROP TRIGGER`, which is itself logged by Postgres. The SHA-256 hash chain on `audit_log` means any tampering that does bypass the trigger leaves a detectable broken link via `verify_chain`. **Corrections are first-class:** instead of ever patching a row, a new `kind='correction'` entry is appended referencing the original row ID. The original stays forever. The three existing `system #0` deploy entries (entity_id was a hardcoded placeholder) were corrected this way — three orange correction entries now live alongside the originals in the changelog. **Supporting fixes shipped this session:** Changelog ID column now truncates full audit SHA-256 hashes to 8 chars for display (full hash still visible on row expand); deploy counter now sequential (`COUNT(*)+1` at startup, not hardcoded 0); Mail and Settings headers moved `+ New lead` and `+ Member` buttons into the `right=` slot (row 1, inline with title); Dashboards sidebar narrowed to a fixed `220px` (was `lg:col-span-1` ~25% of viewport); Gmail Apps Script connector updated for `n.csonka@ternlang.com` (renamed from `csonikoletta`, 2026-06-05) and new `research@ternlang.com` alias. |

| 2026-06-01 · 16:04 | **Rusty Penguin — the live desktop now renders through a from-scratch GPU driver (virtio-gpu brick 3).** The whole UI scans out via virtio-gpu instead of the VBE framebuffer. New `gpudisplay` boot flag → `sys_fb_query` hands the desktop the virtio-gpu **backing in cacheable RAM** (not the uncached MMIO fb), so every draw lands in GPU memory; the desktop's `present`/`present_rows` call new `sys_gpu_flush(y,h)` (#34) → `TRANSFER_TO_HOST_2D` + `RESOURCE_FLUSH` for the band — the device DMAs from RAM and the host GPU scans it out. Flush is a no-op under the VBE path so the call is unconditional and additive (VBE boots untouched). The backing is mapped `USER` so the ring-3 desktop renders into it directly (same exposure as the VBE fb). **Verified headless:** QMP screendump of the *virtio-gpu* display (`device=gpu0`) shows the full live desktop — Nebula wallpaper, terminal, dock, ticking clock, cursor — at 1280×800, **no #PF/#GP**. This is the render-to-RAM + DMA-flush model end-to-end: the UI never touches the uncached MMIO framebuffer (the bottleneck behind 1080p drag jank). Proof `docs/virtio-gpu-desktop.png`. Commit 267565c. |
| 2026-06-01 · 15:39 | **Rusty Penguin — from-scratch virtio-gpu driver: transport up + real pixels on the GPU (bricks 1–2).** No virtio code existed in the bare-metal kernel before this. **Brick 1 (6e8fb13):** modern (virtio 1.0) PCI transport from scratch — probe `1AF4:1050`, walk the vendor-cap chain to resolve the common/notify/isr/device structures across BARs (incl. 64-bit BARs) mapped via `vmm::map_mmio_range`; init per spec §3.1.1 (reset→ACK→DRIVER→features `VIRTIO_F_VERSION_1`→FEATURES_OK→DRIVER_OK); a hand-built **split virtqueue** (desc/avail/used rings in fixed identity-mapped DMA RAM past the AHCI scratch zone) with submit+notify+used-ring poll and compiler fences; `GET_DISPLAY_INFO` round-trip → `display 0: 1280x800 (enabled)`. **Brick 2 (b919317):** the 2D display commands `RESOURCE_CREATE_2D → RESOURCE_ATTACH_BACKING → SET_SCANOUT → TRANSFER_TO_HOST_2D → RESOURCE_FLUSH`, plus a scanout self-test that paints the **ternary triad** (warm-red −1 · stone 0 · spring-green +1) into a RAM backing and pushes it through. Verified: screendump of the GPU display = the exact triad at 1280×800, channels correct (`B8G8R8X8` == the desktop's `0x00RRGGBB`). Proof `docs/virtio-gpu-ternary-triad.png`. |
| 2026-06-01 · 15:06 | **Rusty Penguin — user files survive a reboot (RPFS-backed VFS, general persistence).** Disk persistence had been wired only for `.config/` settings; general user files lived in RAM and vanished on reboot. Now **every** user file is mirrored to the on-disk RPFS (syscall 25), and the full working set reloads on boot via a tiny on-disk manifest `.vfsmanifest` (syscall 26 pulls each listed file). Mirroring is armed only *after* the built-in defaults are laid down, so the bulky demo/readme content isn't re-written each boot; delete/rename keep the manifest in sync. **Fixed a reload bug:** the read syscall packs `out_max` into a 16-bit field, so a 65536-byte reload buffer truncated to 0 and read nothing — capped at 65535 (found by grepping the disk image directly: the data *was* on the platter, the reload was the broken half). `launch.sh` now auto-creates `rusty-penguin-disk.img` (256 MiB) and attaches it as a SATA/AHCI drive so persistence works out of the box. **Verified end-to-end across a real reboot** (same disk image): boot1 `echo persistproof42 > probe.txt` → clean power-down; boot2 `cat probe.txt` → `persistproof42`, `ls` lists it; serial: boot1 "RPFS formatted (first boot)" → boot2 "RPFS loaded". Also confirmed the AHCI write self-test passes once a writable disk is attached (the cdrom-only harness couldn't write → the old boot-log "WRITE failed" was a harness artifact). `docs/PERSISTENCE.md`. Commit a295cfa. |

| 2026-06-01 · 14:28 | **Rusty Penguin — real free-list allocator that reclaims memory (was bump-only).** The desktop ran on a bump allocator whose `dealloc` was a no-op: opening/closing windows leaked every allocation, marching the heap toward its 32 MiB ceiling and crashing — the daily-driver stability ceiling. Switched to `current_turn_usage().input_tokens` (the actual context window fill on the most recent API request), which naturally drops after a successful compaction. The same bug + same fix I'd ported from the stale tree — diagnosis was correct, just landed in the wrong file the first time. **(5) Bug fix #2 — mouse/keyboard bleed into input (`albert-cli/tui.rs:3328`):** When slash-command Suspend disabled raw mode + mouse capture for terminal handoff (`/commit`, `/pr`, etc.), mouse movement during that window emitted ANSI escape sequences directly to stdin. On Resume, those bytes were still buffered and the key-event thread parsed them as `Esc + [ + …` fake keystrokes that bled into the input. Added a `while event::poll(Duration::ZERO).unwrap_or(false) { event::read(); }` drain between Resume and key-thread unpause — runs while key thread is still paused, no race. **(6) Bug fix #3 — hardcoded date (`albert-cli/main.rs:102`):** `DEFAULT_DATE = "2024-03-25"` was a compile-time constant. | 2026-05-10 · 15:00 | **MANDELBROT SURGERY — FRACTAL SELF-SIMILAR SYMMETRY BREAK FOR NET2NET**. Replaced Gaussian noise (σ=0.01) in `perform_surgery()` with a Mandelbrot-parameterised perturbation module (`moe-llm-core/src/mandelbrot.rs`). Core idea proposed by Simeon on the road: use the Mandelbrot set's self-similarity property to make cloned layers structurally coherent with each other but individually unique — the same way the fractal boundary looks similar at every zoom level but is never identical. Implementation: for each weight `w` in the cloned layer, construct complex parameter `c = (tanh(w)−0.5) + i·c_im` and run Mandelbrot iteration z_{n+1} = z_n² + c from z=0. The escape iteration count determines perturbation magnitude — weights mapping to the interior (settled learned features, does not escape) receive near-zero perturbation (2% of base scale); boundary-adjacent weights (plastic, uncertain) receive maximum perturbation. Critically, each surgery layer gets a unique latitude `c_im` via a golden-ratio sequence, maximising spacing between consecutive layers' parameter-space positions. The golden ratio is used deliberately: it gives the maximum minimum spacing, so layer k+1 is always as far as possible from all prior layers in Mandelbrot parameter space. Cross-sections of the Mandelbrot set at different latitudes are self-similar (fractal zooming property) — so the perturbation pattern of layer k is structurally related to layer k-1 without being a copy. Perturbation is fully deterministic — no external RNG, bit-for-bit reproducible. Connection to @sparseskip: 9/12 dormant experts per step are seeded with boundary-adjacent weights that sit at the frontier of what training will push first — a structured head start into productive weight-space territory. 7 unit tests all pass. `MAND` telemetry line added to `dashboard/training.log` at each surgery. Whitepaper updated: `\subsubsection{Mandelbrot Symmetry Break}` in §11.2 with full formula derivation. Committed `aaf4e8909`. |

| 2026-05-09 · 18:55 | **DASHBOARD: GRADIENT NORM COLOR FIXED — WHITE→YELLOW→ORANGE→RED (NO PINK)**. Zabih identified that the per-layer gradient norm bars were rendering intermediate values as pink/salmon — aesthetically wrong and psychologically misleading (pink does not signal pressure). Root cause: the original `gradColor()` function faded both G and B channels simultaneously (`rgb(255, 255*(1-t), 255*(1-t))`), producing pink at t≈0.5. Fix: split the ramp into two segments — segment 1 (t < 0.50) fades B only (white → yellow), segment 2 (t ≥ 0.50) fades G only (yellow → orange → red), B stays 0. Then Zabih further requested a wider white floor: layers with low relative gradient pressure (L0-L3 typically t≈0.27-0.37 in log-scale) should render pure white, only genuinely pressured layers earn color. Added explicit `const floor = 0.45` threshold — below this t value returns `rgb(255,255,255)` unconditionally; above it, the normalized range `tn = (t - floor)/(1 - floor)` drives a clean yellow→orange→red ramp. Result: quiet layers white, moderate layers yellow/orange, the single dominant layer (L5 at global step ~2140-2170) burns pure red. All with no pink anywhere. |

| 2026-05-09 · 09:15 | **DASHBOARD FIX: LOG TRUNCATION RECOVERY ON TRAINING RESTART**. Bug found: when `training.log` is reset on training restart, the browser's `lastBytePos` offset was stuck at the old (large) file position. Every subsequent poll returned HTTP 416, the dashboard silently returned early, and all panels stayed on "WAITING FOR DATA" indefinitely — including the new TLIGHT panel. Root cause: poll code ignored the `Content-Range: bytes */SIZE` header sent in 416 responses. Fix: parse that header on 416; if `SIZE < lastBytePos` the log was truncated — reset to 0 and re-read from scratch on next poll. Dashboard now auto-recovers after any training restart without requiring a hard browser refresh. Committed `60be21272` and pushed. |

Critical bug discovered in `traffic_light.rs`: `TARGET` was set to `1.0/12.0 = 0.0833` but `f_i` (fed from selection masks) measures *selection frequency* — for Top-3/12 that is `3/12 = 0.25`. | 2026-05-07 · 23:45 | **ROUTING ENTROPY LOSS — ANTI-COLLAPSE HARDENING SHIPPED**. Final and most important technical milestone of the session: added routing diversity loss directly into the ternary training loop to prevent the gate Nash-equilibrium collapse that killed the current run. Implementation across three files. **(1) `moe.rs`**: Added `ENTROPY_ACC: RefCell<Option<Tensor>>` thread-local alongside the existing `ROUTING_ACC`. In `MoeBlock::forward()`, immediately after the gate linear (before jitter noise), computes `softmax(gate_logits)` over all 12 experts → `sum(p · log(p + 1e-9))` per token → scalar mean, accumulated across all 3 MoE layers per forward pass. Using pre-noise logits gives cleaner gradient signal through gate weights. `clear_entropy_capture()` and `take_entropy_capture() -> Option<Tensor>` exposed; both exported via `mod.rs`. **(2) `train_bible.rs`**: `clear_entropy_capture()` called before every `model.forward()` for clean per-batch state. Entropy tensor taken after forward, scalar extracted for logging. Both L1 and entropy share the same `aux_active = loss < 8.0` gate — dormant while the model is in collapsed/diverged state (loss ≥ 8.0), activates immediately after surgery rollback restores the E11 checkpoint (~6.95 loss). `entropy_lambda = 0.01` — at uniform routing (ln(12) ≈ 2.485 nats × 3 layers ≈ 7.46 total neg-entropy), contribution is ~0.075 to the loss, roughly 1% of CE — strong enough gradient signal to prevent Nash-equilibrium drift, not enough to destabilise LR. **(3) Telemetry**: `ENTR step=N avg=X.XXXX` logged every 10 batches alongside `ROUTE` — reports neg-entropy per MoE layer in nats; at perfect uniform routing avg ≈ -2.485, at concentrated routing approaches 0. **Why this works**: at uniform collapse the neg-entropy gradient is zero (entropy is maximised at uniform — Nash equilibrium), but the term provides a continuous gradient AWAY from uniform at any sub-maximal entropy state. The loss gate ensures it activates exactly when it can help and disengages exactly when it would harm recovery. Binary rebuilt from scratch, clean compile, no new warnings. Next fire: surgery rollback to E11 → aux_active gates on → entropy term active from first post-surgery batch. |

Three distinct failure cascades identified from telemetry data. **(1) Routing collapse**: ROUTE spread metric 0.026 (step 300, differentiated) → 0.001 (step 14,270, perfectly uniform 1/12). Gate logits converged to a Nash equilibrium where all 12 experts receive equal weight. Cause: gate gradients at 0.12 norm (step 14,100) are dominated by STE quantization noise (~0.10 floor from straight-through estimation on ternary expert weights). The gate itself is F32 but receives gradient signal only through expert outputs, which are ternary — gradient magnitude below noise floor = gate update is random walk. **(2) LR/clipping cascade**: clipped steps: 300 (epoch 1) → 30 (epoch 5) → 10 (epoch 8) → 1 (epoch 13) → 0 (epoch 14+). Loss reversed exactly at epoch 13 (best 6.954, then 7.3+). Root cause: cosine LR decayed from 2e-4 → ~1e-5 by step 6,500, dropping gradient magnitudes below MAX_GRAD_NORM=1.0 — clipping stopped. Clipping had been acting as an implicit damping regularizer; without it, the optimizer drifted. Implication: the LR cycle is too short (500 steps) for the 256H model — LR bottoms out before the model has learned anything stable. **(3) Sparsity evolution was healthy**: L0 20%→29%, L1 40%→49%, L2 60%→69% — per-layer gradient matches design (early layers dense, deep layers sparse). The sparsity pattern is correct; the routing is not. EvolutionManager surgery trigger: COLLAPSE_THRESHOLD=8.5 + COLLAPSE_STREAK_LIMIT=2 — current loss at 8.17–8.21 is close; once it crosses 8.5 for 2 consecutive epochs, rollback fires to best checkpoint (E11, Global ~88, loss 6.954).
